{\mathbf{E}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{[E]}}[\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{E+1}-{\mathbf{w}}^{*}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}]\leq\frac{2\epsilon}{\beta},

and further applying
\beta
-smoothness of
f
, we get:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{[E]}}[f({\mathbf{w}}_{E+1})-f({\mathbf{w}}^{*})]\leq{\mathbf{E}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{[E]}}[\frac{\beta}{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{E+1}-{\mathbf{w}}^{*}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}]\leq\epsilon,

which proves the correctness of
Algorithm

2
for the choice of total number of epochs
k_{\epsilon}=E
.

Proof of Sample Complexity.
In order to verify that
Algorithm

2
indeed converges to an
\epsilon
-optimal point in
O(\epsilon^{-2p})
sample complexity, note we simply need to count the total sample complexity incurred in the
k_{\epsilon}
epochwise runs of
Relative-Gradient-Descent
(see Line #5 of
Algorithm

2
). However, by design of
Epoch-RGD
(
Algorithm

2
), since
Relative-Gradient-Descent
\big({\mathbf{w}}_{k},\eta_{k},\gamma_{k},t_{k}\big)
is run for only
t_{k}=\frac{1}{2B^{2}{(D_{k}^{2})}^{2p}}
iterations, the total sample complexity of
Epoch-RGD
becomes:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{k_{\epsilon}}t_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2B^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{\epsilon}}\frac{1}{{(D_{k}^{2})}^{2p}}=\frac{1}{2B^{2}}\bigg(\frac{1}{{(D_{1}^{2})}^{2p}}+\frac{1}{{(D_{2}^{2})}^{2p}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{{(D_{k_{\epsilon}}^{2})}^{2p}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2B^{2}}\bigg(\frac{1}{{(D^{2})}^{2p}}+\frac{1}{{(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}D^{2})}^{2p}}+\frac{1}{{((\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2}D^{2})}^{2p}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{{((\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{k_{\epsilon}-1}D_{k_{\epsilon}}^{2})}^{2p}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2B^{2}(D^{2})^{2p}}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2p}}+\frac{1}{{((\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2}}^{2p}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{{((\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{k_{\epsilon}-1})}^{2p}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2B^{2}(D^{2})^{2p}}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2p}}+\frac{1}{((\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2p})^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{((\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2p})^{k_{\epsilon}-1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2B^{2}(D^{2})^{2p}}\frac{(\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}^{2p})^{k_{\epsilon}}-1}{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}^{2p}-1}\leq\frac{1}{4B^{2}(D^{2})^{2p}}\Big((\nicefrac{{\beta D^{2}}}{{2\epsilon}})^{2p}-1\Big)=O\Big(\frac{1}{B^{2}\epsilon^{2p}}\Big)

where the last inequality is since
k_{\epsilon}=\lceil\log_{4/3}(\frac{\beta D^{2}}{2\epsilon})\rceil
by definition.
Thus follows the claimed sample complexity of
Epoch-RGD
in
6
and this concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix E
Standard Results from Convex Optimization

The results covered in this section can be found in
Hazan 2019
;
Luenberger et al. 1984
;
Boyd et al. 2004
;
Fletcher 2013
;
Nesterov 2003
;
Nocedal and Wright 1999
.

Definition 12

(Lipschitz Function)
.

Assume
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be bounded decision space. Then any function
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
f is called
L
-Lipschitz over
{\mathcal{D}}
with respect to a norm
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\cdot}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
if for all
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, we have:

\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{y}})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\leq L\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}.

E.1
Useful properties for Convex Functions

Definition 13

(Convex Function)
.

Assume
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be any convex and bounded decision space. Then any differential function
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
is called convex if for all
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,

f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{y}})\geq\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})^{\top}({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}).

Lemma 14

(First Order Optimality Condition
(
Luenberger et al. 1984
;
Boyd et al. 2004
)
)
.

Assume
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
is a convex function and
{\mathbf{x}}^{*}
be the minimizer of
f
. Then for any
{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,

\nabla f({\mathbf{x}}^{*})^{\top}({\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{x}}^{*})\geq 0

E.2
Useful properties for
\beta
-Smooth Convex Functions

Definition 15

(
\beta
-Smooth Convex Function)
.

Assume
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be any convex and bounded decision space. Then any differential and convex function
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
is also called
\beta
-smooth (any
\beta>0
) if for all
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,

f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{y}})\leq\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})^{\top}({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}})+\frac{\beta}{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}.

Lemma 16

(Properties of
\beta
-smooth functions
(
Hazan 2019
;
Bubeck 2014
)
)
.

Suppose
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
is a
\beta
-smooth convex function. Then for all
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{y}})\leq\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})^{\top}({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\beta}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})-\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})-\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}.

Further if
{\mathbf{x}}^{*}
is the minimizer of
f
and
{\mathbf{x}}^{*}\in\text{Int}({\mathcal{D}})
(i.e.
{\mathbf{x}}^{*}
belong to the interior of
f
’s domain
{\mathcal{D}}
), then

\displaystyle\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}\leq 2\beta(f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{x}}^{*})\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{x}}^{*}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}.

E.3
Useful properties for
\alpha
-Strongly Convex Functions

Definition 17

(
\alpha
-Strongly Convex Function)
.

Assume
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be any convex and bounded decision space. Then any differential and convex function
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
is also called
\alpha
-strongly convex (any
\alpha>0
) if for all
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,

f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{y}})\geq\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})^{\top}({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}})+\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}.

Lemma 18
.

If
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
is an
\alpha
-strongly convex function, with
{\mathbf{x}}^{*}
being the minimizer of
f
. Then for any
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})-\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\geq\alpha\|{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}\|

\displaystyle\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\nabla f({\mathbf{z}})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\geq\alpha\|{\mathbf{z}}-{\mathbf{x}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{2}\|{\mathbf{x}}^{*}-{\mathbf{x}}\|^{2}\leq f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{x}}^{*}).

Proof.

This simply follows by the properties of
\alpha
-strongly convex function. Note by definition of
\alpha
-strong convexity, for any
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

f({\mathbf{x}})-f({\mathbf{y}})\geq\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})^{\top}({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}})+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}\|^{2}.

Similarly,

f({\mathbf{y}})-f({\mathbf{x}})\geq\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})^{\top}({\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{x}})+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}\|^{2}.

Adding we get:

\displaystyle(\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})-\nabla f({\mathbf{x}}))^{\top}({\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{x}})\geq{\alpha}\|{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}\|^{2}

Now applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the left hand side of the above inequality yields the first result.

To get the second result, let us use
{\mathbf{y}}={\mathbf{z}}
and
{\mathbf{x}}={\mathbf{x}}^{*}
in the above inequality, which along with the first order optimality yields (
14
):

\nabla f({\mathbf{z}})^{\top}({\mathbf{z}}-{\mathbf{x}}^{*})\geq{\alpha}\|{\mathbf{z}}-{\mathbf{x}}^{*}\|^{2}

The result now follows by again applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the left hand side of the above inequality.
Finally the last part of the proof simply follows setting
{\mathbf{y}}={\mathbf{x}}^{*}
and from the first order optimality condition (see
14
).
∎

E.4
Useful properties for
\alpha
-Strongly Convex and
\beta
-Smooth Convex Functions
(\beta\geq\alpha)

Lemma 19

(Properties of
\beta
-smooth and
\alpha
-strongly convex functions
(
Bubeck 2014
)
)
.

Suppose
f:{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
is a
\beta
-smooth and
\alpha
-strongly convex function. Then for all
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle\big(\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})-\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})\big)^{\top}({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}})\geq\frac{\alpha\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\nabla f({\mathbf{x}})-\nabla f({\mathbf{y}})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}
